Allscripts was founded in 1981 and is headquartered in Chicago, Illinois.\n\n## Major Mergers and Acquisitions\n\nIn 2008, Allscripts merged with the healthcare systems operations of its rival, Misys. In 2010, Allscripts-Misys merged with another healthcare information technology competitor, Eclipsys, in a $1.3 billion deal.\n\n## Practice Fusion Acquisition\n\nAllscripts acquired Practice Fusion for $100 million, announced on January 8, 2018. Practice Fusion, founded in 2005 and based in San Francisco, supported 30,000 ambulatory practices and 5 million patient visits a month.\n\nHowever, the acquisition soon became complicated. Allscripts acquired Practice Fusion for $100 Million in 2018, but soon after agreed to pay a $145 Million to resolve potential liabilities related to a Department of Justice investigation into Practice Fusion's business.\n\n## Name Change to Veradigm\n\nThe company formally changed its name from Allscripts to Veradigm in January 2023. In 2010, Allscripts-Misys merged with another healthcare information technology competitor, Eclipsys, in a $1.3 billion deal.\n\nThe Veradigm brand was first formally introduced in November 2018, not as a new company, but as the dedicated branding for Allscripts' Payer & Life Sciences division. On January 3, 2023, Allscripts announced a corporate name change to Veradigm.\n\n## Key Acquisitions\n\nIn March 2013, Allscripts acquired dbMotion, Ltd., a developer of system interfaces and data analytics tools, and Jardogs LLC, a developer of a personal health record. In July 2014, Allscripts acquired the London-based Oasis Medical Solutions to incorporate its leading Patient Administration System into its Allscripts Sunrise clinical system. In 2018 Allscripts acquired Practice Fusion. In 2019, Allscripts acquired ZappRx, a Boston-based prescription drug startup, which provides an online platform used by patients, physicians, and pharmacists for the management of specialty medications.\n\n## Products and Services\n\nVeradigm Inc. is a publicly traded American company that provides physician practices, hospitals, and other healthcare providers with practice management and electronic health record (EHR) technology. Veradigm also provides products for patient engagement and care coordination, as well as financial and analytics technology. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_015TRQ9prPYAp2iDuPz5yABQ","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→# Veradigm — Product Research\n     2→\n     3→Researched: 2026-02-16\n     4→Developer website: https://www.veradigm.com/\n     5→\n     6→## Overview\n     7→\n     8→Veradigm Inc. is the successor entity to Allscripts Healthcare Solutions, a major health IT company founded in the 1980s in Chicago. In May 2022, Allscripts sold its hospital and large physician practice business (Sunrise, Paragon, TouchWorks, dbMotion) to Harris Computer / Constellation Software for ~$700M; that segment became **Altera Digital Health**. The remaining ambulatory EHR, practice management, patient engagement, and data analytics businesses were formally rebranded as **Veradigm** in January 2023.\n     9→\n    10→Veradigm has ~2,450 employees and generates approximately $585M in annual revenue across two segments: Provider (~77% of revenue, covering EHR, practice management, patient engagement, and revenue cycle management) and Payer & Life Sciences (~23%, covering data analytics and real-world evidence). The company claims over 180,000 physician users. It holds approximately 3.6% of the U.S. ambulatory EHR market — significant in the small-to-mid-size independent practice segment, though small relative to Epic (~44%).\n    11→\n    12→Veradigm has been in financial turmoil since March 2023 when it disclosed a ~$20M revenue overstatement (2021-2022), triggering an SEC investigation, executive departures, and Nasdaq delisting in February 2024. Shares now trade OTC under ticker MDRX. The company continues operating and plans to relist after completing financial restatements, targeted for 2026. Despite the turmoil, Veradigm acquired Koha Health (RCM, orthopedic focus) in early 2024 and ScienceIO (AI data platform) for $140M.\n    13→\n    14→Notably, Veradigm also owns **Practice Fusion** (a separate, simpler cloud-native EHR for small practices) and **FollowMyHealth** (a patient engagement platform). These are distinct ONC-certified products. This report focuses on **Veradigm EHR**, the flagship ambulatory EHR product.\n    15→\n    16→## Product: Veradigm EHR\n    17→\n    18→CHPL ID: 11763\n    19→\n    20→### What It Is\n    21→\n    22→Veradigm EHR is the direct successor to **Allscripts Professional EHR** (also known as \"Allscripts Pro\"), which itself descended from the \"HealthMatics\" EHR. It is a full-featured ambulatory EHR targeting small-to-mid-size independent physician practices and multi-location, multi-specialty organizations. It is **not** the same product as Allscripts TouchWorks (which went to Altera Digital Health and serves larger enterprises).\n    23→\n    24→The certified product (Veradigm EHR version 26) has an extensive ONC certification profile with 40+ criteria spanning clinical data management (a)(1)-(a)(15), care coordination (b)(1)-(b)(11), clinical quality measures (c)(1)-(c)(3), patient access (e)(1), public health reporting (f)(1)-(f)(7), FHIR APIs (g)(7)-(g)(10), and Direct messaging (h)(1). This breadth indicates a comprehensive clinical system.\n    25→\n    26→Veradigm EHR is the clinical core, but the full product ecosystem includes **Veradigm Practice Management** (billing/scheduling — separate but integrated product), **FollowMyHealth** (patient portal — EHR-agnostic, separately certified), **Veradigm ePrescribe** (also available standalone), and the **DORN** diagnostic ordering/results network. The certified EHR module is a component of this broader platform.\n    27→\n    28→### Users & Market\n    29→\n    30→**Target users:** Physicians, clinical staff, nurses, and practice administrators in ambulatory settings. The product supports solo practices through multi-site groups with 50+ providers.\n    31→\n    32→**Specialties:** Veradigm claims support for \"nearly every medical specialty\" with preloaded templates and protocols. Explicitly listed specialties include: cardiology, orthopedics, neurology, dermatology, gastroenterology, endocrinology, ENT, urology, family practice, internal medicine, pediatrics, OB/GYN, psychiatry, urgent care, oncology, pulmonology, rheumatology, allergy/immunology, occupational medicine, podiatry, physical therapy, and more.\n    33→\n    34→**Market position:** ~3.6% ambulatory market share. Ranked #1 by Black Book Research (2025) for ambulatory EHR in family practice and primary care. Over 180,000 physician users across the full Veradigm platform (not all on Veradigm EHR specifically — some use Practice Fusion).\n    35→\n    36→**Deployment:** Cloud-based (primary), on-premise, or hybrid. Available via web browser with iOS/Android mobile apps.\n    37→\n    38→**User reviews:** 3.5/5 on Capterra (66 reviews). Users praise customizability, specialty-specific templates, and lab/pharmacy integration. Common complaints include steep learning curve, slow performance (chart loading times), and variable customer support quality.\n    39→\n    40→### Modules & Functionality\n    41→\n    42→Based on Veradigm as a Company\n\n**Veradigm Inc.** (formerly Allscripts Healthcare Solutions, Inc.) is a publicly traded American health IT company headquartered in Chicago, Illinois. It was founded in 1986 (some sources say 1981) as Allscripts Healthcare Solutions.\n\n**Corporate history and key events:**\n\n- **2008**: Allscripts merged with Misys's healthcare systems operations\n- **2010**: Merged with Eclipsys in a $1.3 billion deal, creating one of the largest health IT networks\n- **2013**: Acquired **dbMotion** (interoperability/data analytics) and **Jardogs** (personal health records)\n- **2014**: Acquired Oasis Medical Solutions (UK-based patient administration)\n- **2017**: Purchased NantHealth's patient-engagement assets\n- **2018**: Acquired **Practice Fusion** for $100 million (later paid $145M DOJ settlement for Practice Fusion's pre-acquisition kickback/opioid issues)\n- **2019**: Acquired ZappRx (specialty medications)\n- **2020**: Sold EPSi business ($365M) and CarePort Health ($1.35B)\n- **May 2022**: **Sold hospital/large-practice segment** (Sunrise, Paragon, TouchWorks, Opal, dbMotion) to Harris Computer/Constellation Software for ~$700M. That business became **Altera Digital Health**.\n- **January 2023**: Formally renamed from Allscripts to **Veradigm Inc.**\n- **March 2023**: Disclosed ~$20M revenue overstatement, triggering SEC investigation and executive departures\n- **January 2024**: Acquired Koha Health (RCM, orthopedic focus) and ScienceIO ($140M, AI platform)\n- **February 2024**: **Delisted from Nasdaq** due to inability to file financial reports; now trades OTC as MDRX\n\n**Size:** Approximately 2,450 employees, ~$585M annual revenue. Over 180,000 physician users. Approximately 3.1-3.6% of the U.S. ambulatory EHR market (ranked 7th by Definitive Healthcare with 2,580 installations).\n\n**Current business segments:**\n- **Provider (~77% revenue):** EHR, practice management, patient engagement, revenue cycle\n- **Payer & Life Sciences (~23%):** Data analytics, real-world evidence\n\n---\n\n### 2. It was founded in 1986 (some sources say 1981) as Allscripts Healthcare Solutions.\n\n**Corporate history and key events:**\n\n- **2008**: Allscripts merged with Misys's healthcare systems operations\n- **2010**: Merged with Eclipsys in a $1.3 billion deal, creating one of the largest health IT networks\n- **2013**: Acquired **dbMotion** (interoperability/data analytics) and **Jardogs** (personal health records)\n- **2014**: Acquired Oasis Medical Solutions (UK-based patient administration)\n- **2017**: Purchased NantHealth's patient-engagement assets\n- **2018**: Acquired **Practice Fusion** for $100 million (later paid $145M DOJ settlement for Practice Fusion's pre-acquisition kickback/opioid issues)\n- **2019**: Acquired ZappRx (specialty medications)\n- **2020**: Sold EPSi business ($365M) and CarePort Health ($1.35B)\n- **May 2022**: **Sold hospital/large-practice segment** (Sunrise, Paragon, TouchWorks, Opal, dbMotion) to Harris Computer/Constellation Software for ~$700M. That business became **Altera Digital Health**.\n- **January 2023**: Formally renamed from Allscripts to **Veradigm Inc.**\n- **March 2023**: Disclosed ~$20M revenue overstatement, triggering SEC investigation and executive departures\n- **January 2024**: Acquired Koha Health (RCM, orthopedic focus) and ScienceIO ($140M, AI platform)\n- **February 2024**: **Delisted from Nasdaq** due to inability to file financial reports; now trades OTC as MDRX\n\n**Size:** Approximately 2,450 employees, ~$585M annual revenue. Over 180,000 physician users. Approximately 3.1-3.6% of the U.S. ambulatory EHR market (ranked 7th by Definitive Healthcare with 2,580 installations).\n\n**Current business segments:**\n- **Provider (~77% revenue):** EHR, practice management, patient engagement, revenue cycle\n- **Payer & Life Sciences (~23%):** Data analytics, real-world evidence\n\n---\n\n### 2. What is \"Veradigm View\" Specifically?\n\n**Veradigm View is the rebranded name for Practice Fusion EHR under Veradigm's ONC certification.**\n\nThis is a critical finding. The product documentation, EHI export pages, and internal analysis all confirm that \"Veradigm View\" is the new certified product name for what was previously marketed as **Practice Fusion EHR**. Key evidence:\n\n- The EHI export documentation at `https://veradigm.com/legal/veradigm-view-ehi-export-documentation/v6/` internally references \"Practice Fusion EHR\" in entity descriptions (confirmed in `/home/jmandel/hobby/ehi-export-analysis/results/veradigm--veradigm-ehr/downloads/enrichment/README.md`, line 25: \"The pages reference 'Practice Fusion EHR' -- Veradigm View appears to be the rebranded documentation for Practice Fusion's EHI export, not the Veradigm EHR product.\")\n- The existing analysis file at `/home/jmandel/hobby/ehi-export-analysis/hx/round1/results/veradigm/analysis.json` explicitly states: \"Veradigm View (formerly Practice Fusion)\" and \"Veradigm View (Practice Fusion EHR)\"\n- The EHI export report at `/home/jmandel/hobby/ehi-export-analysis/results/veradigm--veradigm-ehr/ehi-export-report.md` (line 121) confirms: \"The View documentation pages reference 'Practice Fusion EHR' in entity descriptions. Veradigm acquired Practice Fusion in 2018 and appears to have rebranded it as 'Veradigm View.' This is a separate product from Veradigm EHR (formerly Allscripts Professional).\"\n- The login URL is `https://app.veradigmview.com/apps/ehr/index.html` (page title: \"Veradigm View - EHR Login\")\n- There is a separate customer community at `https://help.veradigmview.com/`\n\n**CHPL certification details** (from `/home/jmandel/hobby/ehi-export-analysis/results/veradigm--veradigm-view/chpl-metadata.json`):\n- **CHPL ID:** 11697\n- **CHPL Product Number:** 15.04.04.2891.View.01.02.1.250909\n- **Version:** 1\n- **Certification Date:** 2025-09-09\n- **Certification Status:** Active\n- **Developer:** Veradigm (developer ID 2891)\n\n**Certified criteria** (32 criteria covering a comprehensive ambulatory EHR):\n- **(a) Clinical data:** (a)(1)-(a)(5), (a)(12), (a)(14), (a)(15) -- CPOE, clinical decision support, demographics, problem lists, medication lists, clinical notes, implantable devices, social determinants\n- **(b) Care coordination:** (b)(1)-(b)(3), (b)(10), (b)(11) -- transitions of care, clinical information reconciliation, e-prescribing, EHI export, care plans\n- **(c) Clinical quality:** (c)(1)-(c)(3) -- CQM capture, export, reporting\n- **(d) Privacy/security:** Full suite (d)(1)-(d)(9), (d)(12), (d)(13)\n- **(e) Patient access:** (e)(1), (e)(3) -- patient portal, patient health info export\n- **(f) Public health:** (f)(1), (f)(5) -- immunization registry, cancer registry\n- **(g) APIs:** (g)(2)-(g)(7), (g)(9), (g)(10) -- FHIR R4 APIs, automated access, standardized APIs\n\n**Notable differences from Veradigm EHR (CHPL 11763):** Veradigm View lacks certifications for (b)(7)-(b)(9) (data segmentation, security labeling), (f)(2) (syndromic surveillance), (f)(4) (electronic case reporting), (f)(7) (antimicrobial), (g)(1) (automated numerator recording), and (h)(1) (Direct messaging). This aligns with Practice Fusion being a simpler, cloud-first product.\n\n---\n\n### 3. Who Uses It? What Settings?\n\n**Veradigm View (Practice Fusion)** targets:\n- **Small, independent physician practices** -- solo practitioners and small clinics\n- **Ambulatory/outpatient settings** exclusively (not hospitals or inpatient)\n- Practices seeking \"an intuitive, out-of-the-box solution that operates effortlessly and requires no dedicated IT support\"\n- Over **31,000 clinicians** use it, serving approximately **5 million patient visits per month**\n\n**Deployment:** 100% cloud-based. No on-premise option. Accessible via web browser on any device.\n\n**Market positioning:** This is the simpler, more affordable EHR in Veradigm's portfolio, complementary to **Veradigm EHR** (formerly Allscripts Professional) which targets larger, multi-specialty organizations requiring advanced customization.\n\n---\n\n### 4. {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-6","id":"msg_01FepyAeCbbdhjs6YrxpWFSV","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_0198K2CjgxMYWrQE1x9UmpmY","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/home/jmandel/hobby/ehi-export-analysis/results/veradigm--veradigm-view/product-research.md","content":"# Veradigm — Product Research (Veradigm View)\n\nResearched: 2026-02-16\nDeveloper website: https://www.veradigm.com/\n\n## Overview\n\nVeradigm Inc. (formerly Allscripts Healthcare Solutions, Inc.) is a health IT company headquartered in Chicago, Illinois, with approximately 2,450 employees and ~$585M in annual revenue. The company rebranded from Allscripts to Veradigm in January 2023. In May 2022, Allscripts sold its hospital/large-practice segment (Sunrise, Paragon, TouchWorks, Opal, dbMotion) to Harris Computer/Constellation Software for ~$700M; that business became Altera Digital Health. What remained — ambulatory EHR, e-prescribing, data analytics, life sciences — became Veradigm.\n\nVeradigm's current business has two segments: Provider (~77% of revenue, including EHR, practice management, patient engagement, and revenue cycle) and Payer & Life Sciences (~23%, data analytics and real-world evidence). The company has been through significant turbulence — a $20M revenue overstatement disclosed in March 2023 triggered SEC investigation, executive departures, and eventual Nasdaq delisting in February 2024 (now trades OTC as MDRX). The company claims over 180,000 physician users and holds roughly 3.1–3.6% of the U.S. ambulatory EHR market (7th place per Definitive Healthcare, with ~2,580 installations).\n\nVeradigm acquired Practice Fusion in 2018 for $100M. Practice Fusion was a pioneering cloud-based EHR that had offered a free, ad-supported model before switching to a subscription model. (Allscripts/Veradigm later paid a $145M DOJ settlement related to Practice Fusion's pre-acquisition kickback/opioid marketing issues.)\n\n## Product: Veradigm View\n\nCHPL ID: 11697 (15.04.04.2891.View.01.02.1.250909)\n\n### What It Is\n\n**Veradigm View is the ONC-certified product name for Practice Fusion EHR.** On the Veradigm marketing site and in customer-facing contexts, the product is still called \"Practice Fusion EHR.\" The name \"Veradigm View\" appears specifically in ONC certification and EHI export documentation. Evidence for this identity:\n\n- The EHI export documentation for Veradigm View internally references \"Practice Fusion EHR\" in entity descriptions\n- The login URL is `app.veradigmview.com` with a dedicated customer community at `help.veradigmview.com`\n- The Veradigm EHR Solutions page lists two products: \"Veradigm EHR\" (formerly Allscripts Professional) and \"Practice Fusion EHR\" — there is no third product called \"Veradigm View\"\n- The Veradigm login page lists a \"Practice Fusion\" login, not \"Veradigm View\"\n\nPractice Fusion / Veradigm View is a 100% cloud-based ambulatory EHR designed for small, independent physician practices. It is positioned as \"a simple, intuitive EHR\" offering \"quick integration and immediate operational efficiency\" that \"operates effortlessly and requires no dedicated IT support.\" It is a completely separate product from Veradigm EHR (CHPL 11763, formerly Allscripts Professional), which targets larger multi-specialty organizations with advanced customization needs. The two products have different codebases, different login portals, different data models, and different EHI export formats (View exports TSV files; EHR exports JSON).\n\nThe product is certified for 32 ONC criteria covering clinical documentation (a)(1)-(a)(5), (a)(12), (a)(14)-(a)(15), care coordination (b)(1)-(b)(3), (b)(10)-(b)(11), clinical quality measures (c)(1)-(c)(3), patient access (e)(1), (e)(3), public health reporting (f)(1), (f)(5), and FHIR APIs (g)(7), (g)(9), (g)(10). Compared to Veradigm EHR, it lacks certifications for data segmentation (b)(7)-(b)(9), syndromic surveillance (f)(2), electronic case reporting (f)(4), antimicrobial reporting (f)(7), and Direct messaging (h)(1) — consistent with Practice Fusion being a simpler product.\n\n### Users & Market\n\nPractice Fusion / Veradigm View targets solo practitioners and small clinics seeking an affordable, easy-to-use, cloud-based EHR with minimal IT overhead. Veradigm reports over 31,000 clinicians use it, serving approximately 5 million patient visits per month.\n\nOn Capterra, Practice Fusion has a 3.7/5 rating based on 429 reviews. Users appreciate the user-friendly interface and affordability. Criticisms include limited customization options, rigid workflows, and inconsistent customer support. The product is popular with small to medium-sized practices that prioritize simplicity over advanced configurability.\n\n### Modules & Functionality\n\nBased on the Veradigm website, EHI export documentation (87 TSV entity types in v6), third-party reviews, and ONC certification criteria:\n\n**Clinical Documentation & Charting**\n- Customizable templates for various medical forms and documentation (per vendor marketing and Capterra reviews)\n- Encounter documentation with assessments, assessment plans, diagnoses, procedures, observations, events, medications, and addendums (per EHI export entity list)\n- Clinical worksheet summaries and details\n- Problem lists / conditions and diagnoses\n- Patient education records\n- Advance directives\n- Health concerns and goals\n- Care addition)\n\n**Analytics & Reporting**\n- Analytics and reporting tools for monitoring practice performance and patient outcomes (per vendor marketing)\n- Clinical quality measure capture, export, and (formerly Allscripts Healthcare Solutions, Inc.) is a health IT company headquartered in Chicago, Illinois, with approximately 2,450 employees and ~$585M in annual revenue. The company rebranded from Allscripts to Veradigm in January 2023. In May 2022, Allscripts sold its hospital/large-practice segment (Sunrise, Paragon, TouchWorks, Opal, dbMotion) to Harris Computer/Constellation Software for ~$700M; that business became Altera Digital Health. What remained — ambulatory EHR, e-prescribing, data analytics, life sciences — became Veradigm.\n\nVeradigm's current business has two segments: Provider (~77% of revenue, including EHR, practice management, patient engagement, and revenue cycle) and Payer & Life Sciences (~23%, data analytics and real-world evidence). The company has been through significant turbulence — a $20M revenue overstatement disclosed in March 2023 triggered SEC investigation, executive departures, and eventual Nasdaq delisting in February 2024 (now trades OTC as MDRX). The company claims over 180,000 physician users and holds roughly 3.1–3.6% of the U.S. ambulatory EHR market (7th place per Definitive Healthcare, with ~2,580 installations).\n\nVeradigm acquired Practice Fusion in 2018 for $100M. Practice Fusion was a pioneering cloud-based EHR that had offered a free, ad-supported model before switching to a subscription model. (Allscripts/Veradigm later paid a $145M DOJ settlement related to Practice Fusion's pre-acquisition kickback/opioid marketing issues.)\n\n## Product: Veradigm View\n\nCHPL ID: 11697 (15.04.04.2891.View.01.02.1.250909)\n\n### What It Is\n\n**Veradigm View is the ONC-certified product name for Practice Fusion EHR.** On the Veradigm marketing site and in customer-facing contexts, the product is still called \"Practice Fusion EHR.\" The name \"Veradigm View\" appears specifically in ONC certification and EHI export documentation. Evidence for this identity:\n\n- The EHI export documentation for Veradigm View internally references \"Practice Fusion EHR\" in entity descriptions\n- The login URL is `app.veradigmview.com` with a dedicated customer community at `help.veradigmview.com`\n- The Veradigm EHR Solutions page lists two products: \"Veradigm EHR\" (formerly Allscripts Professional) and \"Practice Fusion EHR\" — there is no third product called \"Veradigm View\"\n- The Veradigm login page lists a \"Practice Fusion\" login, not \"Veradigm View\"\n\nPractice Fusion / Veradigm View is a 100% cloud-based ambulatory EHR designed for small, independent physician practices. It is positioned as \"a simple, intuitive EHR\" offering \"quick integration and immediate operational efficiency\" that \"operates effortlessly and requires no dedicated IT support.\" It is a completely separate product from Veradigm EHR (CHPL 11763, formerly Allscripts Professional), which targets larger multi-specialty organizations with advanced customization needs. The two products have different codebases, different login portals, different data models, and different EHI export formats (View exports TSV files; EHR exports JSON).\n\nThe product is certified for 32 ONC criteria covering clinical documentation (a)(1)-(a)(5), (a)(12), (a)(14)-(a)(15), care coordination (b)(1)-(b)(3), (b)(10)-(b)(11), clinical quality measures (c)(1)-(c)(3), patient access (e)(1), (e)(3), public health reporting (f)(1), (f)(5), and FHIR APIs (g)(7), (g)(9), (g)(10). Compared to Veradigm EHR, it lacks certifications for data segmentation (b)(7)-(b)(9), syndromic surveillance (f)(2), electronic case reporting (f)(4), antimicrobial reporting (f)(7), and Direct messaging (h)(1) — consistent with Practice Fusion being a simpler product.\n\n### Users & Market\n\nPractice Fusion / Veradigm View targets solo practitioners and small clinics seeking an affordable, easy-to-use, cloud-based EHR with minimal IT overhead. Veradigm reports over 31,000 clinicians use it, serving approximately 5 million patient visits per month.\n\nOn Capterra, Practice Fusion has a 3.7/5 rating based on 429 reviews. Users appreciate the user-friendly interface and affordability. Criticisms include limited customization options, rigid workflows, and inconsistent customer support. The product is popular with small to medium-sized practices that prioritize simplicity over advanced configurability.\n\n### Modules & Functionality\n\nBased on the Veradigm website, EHI export documentation (87 TSV entity types in v6), third-party reviews, and ONC certification criteria:\n\n**Clinical Documentation & Charting**\n- Customizable templates for various medical forms and documentation (per vendor marketing and Capterra reviews)\n- Encounter documentation with assessments, assessment plans, diagnoses, procedures, observations, events, medications, and addendums (per EHI export entity list)\n- Clinical worksheet summaries and details\n- Problem lists / conditions and diagnoses\n- Patient education records\n- Advance directives\n- Health concerns and goals\n- Care plans\n- Pinned notes (clinical sticky notes)\n- Smoking status tracking\n- Family history\n- Healthcare device/implant tracking\n- Risk scores (e.g., HCC/RAF)\n\n**Medications & E-Prescribing**\n- Electronic prescribing including controlled substances (EPCS) — certified for (a)(1) CPOE\n- Medication lists and medication history\n- Prescription management and transmission with transaction tracking\n- Drug alert override tracking (clinical decision support)\n- Pharmacy directory and preferred pharmacy management\n- Medication history consent tracking\n- Immunization management with VIS (Vaccine Information Statement) edition tracking\n- Immunization transmission history (registry reporting)\n\n**Laboratory**\n- Lab orders with items, specimens, diagnoses, answers, and order documents\n- Lab results with test observations, notes, observation diagnoses, specimen data, and result documents\n- Electronic lab results integration (per vendor marketing: integration with laboratory systems)\n\n**Billing & Insurance**\n- Integrated billing and claims management described by vendor as \"comprehensive\" with automation to reduce denied claims (per Veradigm website and EMRSystems review)\n- Superbills with associated diagnoses, procedures, modifiers, events, and insurance links (per EHI export entities)\n- Patient insurance information and eligibility verification\n- Guarantor information\n- Financial resource tracking\n- Service restrictions\n\n**Patient Engagement**\n- Patient portal providing access to health records, lab results, and appointment schedules — certified for (e)(1) VDT\n- Patient messaging with attachments and recipients\n- Patient questionnaires\n- Patient documents\n- Free personal health record for patients (per Veradigm marketing)\n- Automated appointment reminders (per EMRSystems review)\n\n**Scheduling**\n- Appointment scheduling (per vendor marketing: \"integrated scheduling\")\n\n**Referrals**\n- Referral management with recipients\n\n**Telemedicine**\n- Telemedicine integration for virtual visits (per Veradigm marketing and third-party reviews; appears to be a more recent addition)\n\n**Analytics & Reporting**\n- Analytics and reporting tools for monitoring practice performance and patient outcomes (per vendor marketing)\n- Clinical quality measure capture, export, and reporting — certified for (c)(1)-(c)(3)\n\n**Interoperability**\n- FHIR R4 API access — certified for (g)(7), (g)(9), (g)(10)\n- Transitions of care / C-CDA — certified for (b)(1)-(b)(3)\n- Immunization registry reporting — certified for (f)(1)\n- Cancer registry reporting — certified for (f)(5)\n- EHI bulk data export in TSV format — certified for (b)(10)\n\n**Administrative**\n- Provider profiles and user management\n- Facility information\n- Care team assignment and profiles\n- Contact profiles\n- Communication settings\n\n### Data & Content\n\nBased on the v6 EHI export documentation (87 TSV files with 1,194 total fields), Veradigm View stores data across these major categories:\n\n| Category | Entity Count | Key Data |\n|---|---|---|\n| Demographics | 10 |
